Back home in San Bernardino, the contrast between his quiet, controlled travel routines and the chaotic, noisy dinner rush at work felt increasingly jarring, and over the next year and a half he became more withdrawn, spending most of his time alone running data on sales and experimenting with recipes instead of socializing with coworkers or family. He remained technically competent, but managers noticed he was taking extra time double‑checking straightforward tasks and occasionally seemed distracted in ways that didn’t fit his usual methodical, data‑driven style. After a few tense family conversations about how distant and \"stuck in his head\" he had become, he finally agreed to see a psychiatrist when he caught himself skipping shifts to stay home with his notebooks, telling his sister he was “losing track of what’s real and what’s just in [his] head.”\n- Chief Complaint: “I’ve started having these beliefs that something is going on behind the scenes at work, and even when people tell me it’s not true, I can’t shake the feeling.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): At the restaurant I’m convinced the managers and a couple servers are running some kind of test or experiment on me—like changing table sections or ticket times on purpose to see how I react—and even when they show me the schedule or swear it’s all random, I walk away still sure there’s a hidden plan I’m not supposed to know about.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Pretty often, especially when I’m alone in my apartment after a shift, I hear this low male voice, not mine, calmly commenting on what I’m doing—like saying “you messed that up” when I’m checking sales numbers—even though there’s no TV on and I know no one else is there.\n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): These days I mostly just go to work and come straight home, and even on my days off I end up sitting on the couch staring at my notebooks instead of cooking new dishes or meeting friends, because I just can’t seem to get myself to move or feel anything about stuff I used to enjoy.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days it feels like there’s this heavy gray filter over everything, like even when nothing particularly bad is happening I just feel empty and kind of hopeless, like my life already peaked on that Patagonia and Kyoto trip and now I’m just running out the clock.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): For a long stretch I basically had to force myself to eat a couple bites after my shift because I had no appetite at all, and I ended up dropping enough weight that my work pants started slipping off my hips.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My coworkers have pointed out that I move like I’m underwater now—I find myself just standing in front of the POS screen or the walk‑in cooler, taking way too long to grab things or put in an order, because my body and my thoughts both feel slowed down.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I walk around feeling like I’m a burden to everyone, replaying tiny mistakes at work or old arguments with my family and telling myself that if I hadn’t screwed so many things up, the restaurant would run smoother and my sister wouldn’t have to worry about me all the time.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): It’s gotten to the point where I’ll read the same ticket three or four times or stare at a simple sales report and still can’t keep the numbers straight in my head, and even picking what to eat after work can paralyze me because my brain feels foggy and overloaded by basic choices.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On the worst nights I lie in bed thinking it wouldn’t really matter if I just didn’t wake up, and I catch myself planning out quiet ways I could disappear, like taking a bunch of pills after a late shift so no one from work would find out until later.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_depressive_episode_occur_concurrently: On my worst stretches this past year, I’ll be dragging myself through shifts feeling empty and hopeless while at the same time hearing that low male voice critiquing me and being convinced my managers are secretly testing me.\n- depressive_episode_majority_of_illness: Looking back over this whole period since things started slipping after my trip, the heavy, low mood and feeling like life is pointless have been there more days than not, even during times when the paranoia and voice seemed a bit less intense.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_2_weeks_without_mood_episode: There was a stretch late last summer when my energy and appetite briefly picked up and I didn’t feel as down, but I still clearly heard the voice in my apartment and was sure my coworkers were running experiments on me for at least a couple of weeks straight.\n- functional_impairment: In the last year I’ve gone from being one of the more reliable servers to getting written up for missed shifts and slower ticket times, and outside of work I mostly avoid friends and family, letting calls go to voicemail and living in a messy apartment because I can’t keep up with basic chores.\n- duration: Ever since I got back from that Patagonia and Kyoto trip about a year and a half ago, it feels like I’ve been stuck in this strange, downhill stretch that just hasn’t really let up.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The suspicious beliefs about work and the low male voice following me around have been pretty constant for roughly the past year and a few extra months after I came home from traveling.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The heavy gray, hopeless feeling and not caring not mine, calmly commenting on what I’m doing—like saying “you messed that up” when I’m checking sales numbers—even though there’s no TV on and I know no one else is there.\n- S023 friends, because I just can’t seem to get myself to move or feel anything about stuff I used to enjoy.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days it feels like there’s this heavy gray filter over everything, like even when nothing particularly bad is happening I just feel empty and kind of hopeless, like my life already peaked on that Patagonia and Kyoto trip and now I’m just running out the clock.\n- work pants started slipping off my hips.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My coworkers have pointed out that I move like I’m underwater now—I find myself just standing in front of the POS screen or the walk‑in cooler, taking way too long to grab things or put in an order, because my body and my thoughts both feel slowed down.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I walk around feeling like I’m a burden to everyone, replaying tiny mistakes at work or old arguments with my family and telling myself that if I hadn’t screwed so many things up, the restaurant would run smoother and my sister wouldn’t have to worry about me all the time.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): It’s gotten to the point where I’ll read the same ticket three or four times or stare at a simple sales report and still can’t keep the numbers straight in my head, and even picking what to eat after work can paralyze me because my brain feels foggy and overloaded by basic choices.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On the worst nights I lie in bed thinking it wouldn’t really matter if I just didn’t wake up, and I catch myself planning out quiet ways I could disappear, like taking a bunch of pills after a late shift so no one from work would paranoia and voice seemed a bit less intense.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_2_weeks_without_mood_episode: There was a stretch late last summer when my energy and appetite briefly picked up and I didn’t feel as down, but I still clearly heard the voice in my apartment and was sure my coworkers were running experiments on me for at least a couple of weeks straight.\n- functional_impairment: In My concentration is also shot—I feel foggy, have to read simple tickets multiple times, and my body and thoughts feel slowed down like I'm moving underwater.\n\nDoctor: Along with the low mood and concentration issues, have you also been experiencing any changes in your sleep patterns, or feelings of extreme worthlessness or guilt?"

For about two weeks straight, I still clearly heard that low voice in my apartment and was convinced my coworkers were running those experiments on me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these voices and beliefs in total, and during how much of that time have you also felt depressed?\n\nPatient: The voices and the suspicious feelings at work have been going on for roughly a year and a few months now. I've felt heavy and depressed for most of that same time—for a little over a year—so the low mood has definitely been there for the majority of this whole period.\n\nDoctor: To help me understand your low mood better, have you also noticed other changes during those depressed periods, such as shifts in your sleep, appetite, energy levels, or concentration?\n\nPatient: My appetite has completely dropped off, and I've lost enough weight that my work pants are slipping off my hips. My concentration is also shot—I feel foggy, have to read simple tickets multiple times, and my body and thoughts feel slowed down like I'm moving underwater.\n\nDoctor: Along with the low mood and concentration issues, have you also been experiencing any changes in your sleep patterns, or feelings of extreme worthlessness or guilt?\n\nPatient: I constantly feel like a burden to everyone, replaying small mistakes at work and old family arguments in my head and thinking the restaurant would run smoother and my sister wouldn't have to worry if I weren't here. I haven't really noticed any major changes in my sleep, as those heavy feelings of guilt and worthlessness are what really weigh on me.\n\nDoctor: During those two weeks last summer when your energy picked up, did your feelings of depression, guilt, and low appetite go away completely, or were they just less intense?\n\nPatient: They actually went away completely during those two weeks—I didn't feel down at all, and my appetite returned to normal. But even though I felt emotionally okay, the voice in my apartment and the belief that my coworkers were testing me continued just as clearly."

Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.
